        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Asset Slots for AI-Generated Sequence Components

## Overview
The current implementation lets AI-generated sequence components declare loose `params.imageAssetKeys` / `params.videoAssetKeys`, then the host conventionally materializes them to `params.images` / `params.videos`. That behavior appears in `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/templates.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/sequence-component-validation.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/CodePathPreview.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/CodePathParamEditor.tsx`, and `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`.

The cleaner fix is to make generated components self-describing with explicit slot metadata, persist that slot contract on `sequence-component` resources, and treat user choices as `params.assetSlotBindings` (or an equivalent single slot-binding param) rather than top-level loose image/video arrays. Existing built-in trusted sequences can keep descriptor-driven `asset-list` params unless the product wants to migrate them too. Backwards compatibility is explicitly not required, so new generated components and saved generated resources should use the slot contract only.

## Phase 1: Define the Contract — Types, Shapes, and Naming

### Step 1: Add shared asset-slot types (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/assetSlots.ts`, `src/features/resources/hooks/useResources.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** a focused asset slot module with types and helpers for:
   - `SequenceAssetSlot`: `{ id, label, mediaType: 'image' | 'video', minItems?, maxItems?, required?, defaultAssetKeys? }`.
   - `SequenceAssetSlotBindings`: `Record<string, string[]>` keyed by slot id.
   - `normalizeAssetSlots`, `normalizeAssetSlotBindings`, and `validateAssetSlotBindings`.
2. **Persist** `assetSlots?: unknown[]` on `SequenceComponentMetadata` in `src/features/resources/hooks/useResources.ts` alongside `controlsManifest`.
3. **Extend** `SequenceComponentResource` flow in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-component-catalog.ts` only if needed by types; the current metadata spread likely carries the new field automatically.

### Step 2: Choose the slot param name and URL materialization shape (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/assetSlots.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Use** `params.assetSlotBindings` as the persisted key-binding object for generated components.
2. **Materialize** to `params.assetSlots` as a runtime object shaped like `Record<slotId, string[]>`, where each array contains resolved URLs in binding order.
3. **Reserve** `assetSlots` as host-injected URL data, not a user-authored schema field.
4. **Document** this in code comments near the contract helpers because the name collision risk is high.

## Phase 2: Edge Generation Contract and Validation

### Step 3: Update prompts to require `ASSET_SLOTS` metadata (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/templates.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** the current asset-key prompt block with a slot-based contract:
   - Metadata lines include `// ASSET_SLOTS: [...]` between `DEFAULTS` and `CONTROLS`.
   - Number allowed assets in the user prompt with media type and label, for example `1. [image] asset-a — Shot name`.
   - Instruct the model to put default selected keys in `DEFAULTS.assetSlotBindings`, not `imageAssetKeys` or `videoAssetKeys`.
   - Instruct render code to read URLs from `params.assetSlots.<slotId>`.
2. **Keep** URL arrays excluded from `CONTROLS`; slot pickers own them.
3. **Remove** prompt guidance that asks for `imageAssetKeys`, `videoAssetKeys`, `images`, or `videos` for generated components.

### Step 4: Parse and return `assetSlots` from generated code (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/templates.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `ExtractedSequenceComponentMeta` with `assetSlots: unknown[]`.
2. **Require** `ASSET_SLOTS` to parse as a JSON array. Allow `[]` when no media is needed or no allowed assets exist.
3. **Include** `assetSlots` in the edge response shape and frontend service response type.
4. **Fail** generation if `ASSET_SLOTS` is missing, since backwards compatibility is not required for new generated components.

### Step 5: Validate slot metadata and bindings on the edge (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/sequence-component-validation.ts`, new edge helper if useful)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Reject** generated schema/defaults/code that declare or use loose `imageAssetKeys`, `videoAssetKeys`, `images`, or `videos` for generated components.
2. **Validate** every slot has a stable JS-safe `id`, label, media type, sane cardinality, and no duplicate ids.
3. **Validate** `DEFAULTS.assetSlotBindings` references only declared slots and only allowed asset keys of the slot’s media type.
4. **Validate** required slots and `minItems` / `maxItems` against defaults when allowed assets exist.
5. **Allow** code references to `params.assetSlots` and `params.assetSlotBindings` without requiring both to be user-facing controls.

## Phase 3: Frontend Service, Store, Save, and Load

### Step 6: Add generated-component asset slots to frontend types (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/sequenceGenerationService.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/state/sequenceCreatorStore.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `SequenceComponentGenerationResponse`, `RunSequenceComponentGenerationResult`, and `GeneratedComponent` with `assetSlots?: unknown[]`.
2. **Normalize** edge-returned slot metadata client-side before accepting the result.
3. **Keep** current user-edited slot bindings inside `generatedComponent.defaultsJson.assetSlotBindings` so preview, save, load, Insert, and Replace read one source of truth.

### Step 7: Save and load slot metadata with resources (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`, `src/features/resources/hooks/useResources.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Include** `assetSlots` in the resource metadata built by `persistGeneratedComponent`.
2. **When loading or previewing** a library resource, populate `generatedComponent.assetSlots` from the resource.
3. **When reusing** an existing library component for Insert/Replace, use the current in-panel `defaultsJson.assetSlotBindings`, not the original resource defaults, so library slot picker changes apply.
4. **Do not** add MP4 preview cache fields or flows.

## Phase 4: Materialization and Runtime Preview

### Step 8: Replace convention-based generated-component materialization (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** slot-based materialization that resolves `params.assetSlotBindings` from registry keys to `params.assetSlots` URL arrays.
2. **Validate** missing registry entries and wrong media type when registry metadata is available.
3. **Preserve** descriptor-driven materialization for trusted built-in sequence params like `image-jump.imageAssetKeys -> images` unless explicitly migrated later.
4. **Remove** generic convention materialization for loose `imageAssetKeys` / `videoAssetKeys` on custom generated components.

### Step 9: Update code-path preview to use slots (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/CodePathPreview.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Accept** `assetSlots` and current `assetSlotBindings` in preview props.
2. **Resolve** bindings against `allowedAssets` into runtime `params.assetSlots`.
3. **Stop** injecting `imageAssetKeys`, `assetKeys`, `assetUrls`, or `images` fallback for generated components.
4. **Use** the first bound image/video only for `clip.asset` compatibility when the generated component relies on `clip.asset`; otherwise keep media access through `params.assetSlots`.

## Phase 5: Slot Picker UI and Validation

### Step 10: Build a reusable slot picker (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/AssetSlotPicker.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Render** one section per declared slot with media-type filtered candidates from `allowedAssets`.
2. **Support** cardinality by slot: single-select when `maxItems === 1`, multi-select otherwise.
3. **Show** missing required slots and over/under cardinality inline.
4. **Write** changes to `defaultsJson.assetSlotBindings` through the existing generated component state update.

### Step 11: Wire slot pickers into generated and library flows (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Render** the slot picker above `ControlsManifestLayout` / `CodePathParamEditor` for generated components and loaded library components.
2. **Validate** slot bindings before enabling Insert/Replace for generated/library components.
3. **Make** library Preview use the current slot assignments, not raw resource defaults.
4. **Make** Insert/Replace use current slot assignments for both freshly generated and loaded library components.

### Step 12: Keep trusted JSON sequence editing stable (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/SequenceParamEditor.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Leave** built-in trusted asset-list params functional for existing trusted sequence drafts.
2. **Optionally** improve `SequenceParamEditor` asset-list editing to use the same picker component only if it can be done without broadening the slot contract to built-ins.

## Phase 6: Tests and Verification

### Step 13: Add edge tests (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/sequence-component-validation.test.ts`, template extraction tests if present)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Assert** valid `ASSET_SLOTS` plus `DEFAULTS.assetSlotBindings` passes.
2. **Assert** missing `ASSET_SLOTS`, duplicate slots, wrong media type, over max, under min, unknown slot id, and unknown asset key fail.
3. **Assert** loose `imageAssetKeys` / `videoAssetKeys` / `images` / `videos` are rejected for generated components.

### Step 14: Add frontend unit tests (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/CodePathPreview` tests if practical)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Test** `assetSlotBindings` materializes to `assetSlots` URLs without mutating persisted params.
2. **Test** unresolved or wrong-media bindings surface validation failures.
3. **Test** Sequence Creator generated-component slot picker changes update preview and are used by Insert/Replace.
4. **Test** loading a library component preserves `assetSlots` metadata and Insert/Replace uses edited bindings.
5. **Retire** tests that expect generated components to use loose image/video asset key conventions.

### Step 15: Run focused validation first, then broader checks
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** targeted frontend tests first:
   ```bash
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx
   ```
2. **Run** Deno edge tests for the generated sequence component function:
   ```bash
   deno test supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/
   ```
3. **Run** typecheck/build-level validation:
   ```bash
   npm run build
   ```
4. **Run** lints if time permits after targeted behavior is green:
   ```bash
   npm run lint
   npm run lint:edge
   ```

## Execution Order
1. Define the shared slot contract and metadata types before changing prompts or UI.
2. Update edge prompts/parsing/validation next so new generations produce the right shape.
3. Wire frontend service/store/save/load after the response shape is stable.
4. Add materialization and preview support before enabling UI slot pickers.
5. Add validation gates before enabling Insert/Replace with generated or library components.
6. Update tests alongside each layer, with old [REDACTED] assertions removed rather than preserved.

## Validation Order
1. Start with pure helper tests for slot normalization and materialization.
2. Run edge validator tests once `ASSET_SLOTS` parsing is implemented.
3. Run Sequence Creator panel tests for preview, library load, Insert, and Replace behavior.
4. Finish with `npm run build`, then lint commands if the implementation touches broad type surfaces.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 claims `updatepinnedgroupwithchildren` is 'currently imported by useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:357'. verified at useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:71-130 that this function is actually **defined locally** in useswitchtofinalvideo.ts, not imported from pinned-group-projection.ts. the real imports from pinned-group-projection at :5-8 are `buildgroupfromchildren` and `replacegroupchildren`, both of which are already in step 5's delete list. the plan's characterization is factually wrong (delete list shouldn't include updatepinnedgroupwithchildren as a pinned-group-projection export), but the substantive intent still works: once `buildgroupfromchildren`/`replacegroupchildren` are deleted, the local helper won't compile and step 11.2's 'inline soft-tag construction' rewrite removes it. minor wording error, not a blocking issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-132",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11.2 specifies the reverse switch is 'single commit, no `start`/`children`/`offset` reads anywhere' but doesn't state the mutation type explicitly. since the step describes metadeletes + cliporderoverride + pinnedshotgroupsoverride in a single commit, the mutation must be `type: 'rows'` (the only variant carrying all three). self-consistent with step 11.1's restriction on `type: 'pinnedshotgroups'`, but making the mutation type explicit would reduce executor ambiguity.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-135",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 3 against the actual `useselectedmediaclips()` inputs in [useselectedmediaclips.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). that hook currently reads only `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig` from editor context, so the plan's literal instruction to join `resolvedconfig.pinnedshotgroups` with `useshots()` data will not compile unless it first switches to `data?.config.pinnedshotgroups` or another real source of pinned groups.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-149",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual timeline selection ownership across `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineselection.ts`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usemultiselect.ts`. `usetimelinecommit` owns only `selectedclipid` and `selectedtrackid`, while `usemultiselect` still owns `selectedclipids`, `primaryclipid`, and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan's step 3 is technically incomplete: following it literally would leave timeline selection split between the new store and old hook-local state instead of collapsing it into one authoritative store.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-150",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current imperative selection adapters in `useclipdrag.ts`, `usetimelinepersistence.ts`, `usepollsync.ts`, and `videoeditorprovider.tsx`. the mutable refs (`selectedclipidsref`, `additiveselectionref`, `selectedclipidref`, `selectedtrackidref`) are runtime adapters for drag/save/poll callers rather than durable domain state, so the plan's wording about putting 'refs/helpers' into the `timeline` slice is a design error unless it explicitly keeps store state separate from ref adapters rebuilt around that state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

    {
      "id": "DEBT-166",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` and `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/tracklistrenderer.tsx`. `timelinecanvas` still computes shared `rowresizepreview`, `selectedtrackid`, and other drag-sensitive props centrally and passes them through every `sortablerow`, so moving context reads to narrow zustand selectors alone will not meet the gate that non-dragged rows stay at 0 rerenders during drag unless the plan also changes this row-prop fan-out.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app





        Idea:
Implement a clean asset-slots contract for AI-generated Sequence Creator components. Replace loose imageAssetKeys/videoAssetKeys for new generated sequence components with explicit ASSET_SLOTS metadata and slot-based params. Number allowed assets in prompts; save assetSlots/default bindings with resources; materialize slot key bindings to URLs; add Sequence Creator slot pickers for generated/library components; make library preview and Insert/Replace use current slot assignments; validate media type/cardinality/missing assets; update edge templates/validators/frontend parsing/types/materialization/preview/save/load/tests. No MP4 preview caching. Backwards compatibility is not important.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Asset Slots for AI-Generated Sequence Components

## Overview
The root approach is still correct: the issue is not in timeline media assets generally, but in the generated Sequence Creator component contract. The critique shows the first plan was missing several boundary changes required by that contract: generated-code param coverage, both controls-manifest validators, runtime access to saved slot metadata, slot-aware smoke rendering, and a richer edge allowed-asset payload. This revision keeps the scope focused on AI-generated DB-stored sequence components and does not migrate trusted built-in sequence `asset-list` params.

Current generated components use loose `params.imageAssetKeys` / `params.videoAssetKeys`, with frontend convention materialization to `params.images` / `params.videos`. The new contract is: persist `params.assetSlotBindings` as registry-key arrays, persist component-level `assetSlots` metadata on sequence-component resources, and inject runtime `params.assetSlots` as `Record<slotId, string[]>` URL arrays. `assetSlots` is host-injected and must be exempt from user-param coverage and controls-manifest coverage; `assetSlotBindings` is persisted defaults data and slot-picker owned, not a normal control.

## Phase 1: Contract Foundation

### Step 1: Add shared asset-slot helpers (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/assetSlots.ts`, `src/features/resources/hooks/useResources.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `assetSlots.ts` with:
   - `SequenceAssetSlot`: `{ id, label, mediaType: 'image' | 'video', minItems?, maxItems?, required?, defaultAssetKeys? }`.
   - `SequenceAssetSlotBindings`: `Record<string, string[]>`.
   - `ResolvedSequenceAssetSlots`: `Record<string, string[]>` URL arrays.
   - `normalizeAssetSlots`, `normalizeAssetSlotBindings`, `validateAssetSlotBindings`, `materializeAssetSlotBindings`, and `inferRegistryAssetMediaType`.
2. **Infer** registry media type from `ResolvedAssetRegistryEntry.type` MIME-ish strings first, then URL/file extension only as a fallback. In Sequence Creator preview/UI, prefer `AllowedSequenceAsset.mediaType`, because that is already normalized.
3. **Add** `assetSlots?: unknown[]` to `SequenceComponentMetadata` in `src/features/resources/hooks/useResources.ts`.
4. **Keep** trusted built-in sequence descriptors unchanged; this module supports generated DB components first.

### Step 2: Make the slot param contract explicit (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/assetSlots.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Persist** key choices in `params.assetSlotBindings`.
2. **Inject** runtime URLs in `params.assetSlots` as `Record<slotId, string[]>`.
3. **Reserve** both names from user controls: `assetSlots` is host-injected runtime data; `assetSlotBindings` is edited only by slot pickers.
4. **Reject** generated-component use of loose `imageAssetKeys`, `videoAssetKeys`, `images`, and `videos`.

## Phase 2: Edge Prompt, Parsing, and Validators

### Step 3: Pass rich allowed assets to edge prompt/validation (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/index.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/templates.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Normalize** `allowed_assets` on the edge into objects containing `key`, `mediaType`, `label`, and optional source context; keep `allowedAssetKeys` only as a derived convenience.
2. **Number** allowed assets in prompts using the normalized objects, for example `1. [image] asset-a - Shot name`.
3. **Pass** normalized allowed assets into template construction and slot validation so media-type checks do not depend on a flat key list.

### Step 4: Require `ASSET_SLOTS` metadata in generated code (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/templates.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** output requirements to include `// ASSET_SLOTS: [...]` between `DEFAULTS` and `CONTROLS`.
2. **Instruct** the model to declare slots in `ASSET_SLOTS`, put default registry keys in `DEFAULTS.assetSlotBindings`, and render from `params.assetSlots.<slotId>`.
3. **Remove** prompt guidance for generated components that asks for `imageAssetKeys`, `videoAssetKeys`, `images`, or `videos`.
4. **Allow** `ASSET_SLOTS: []` for components that genuinely do not use media or when no allowed media is available.

### Step 5: Parse and return slot metadata (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/templates.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/index.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/sequenceGenerationService.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** extraction metadata and edge response types with `assetSlots`.
2. **Require** `ASSET_SLOTS` to parse as a JSON array for new generated components.
3. **Return** `assetSlots` to the frontend and normalize it before accepting a successful code-path generation.

### Step 6: Update generated-code and controls-manifest validators (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/sequence-component-validation.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/controls-manifest-validation.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/controlsManifest.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Exempt** `params.assetSlots` from SCHEMA/DEFAULTS coverage in `validateSequenceComponentCode()` because it is host-injected runtime data.
2. **Permit** `params.assetSlotBindings` in SCHEMA/DEFAULTS only as persisted binding data, while excluding it from `CONTROLS` cross-coverage.
3. **Add** `assetSlots` and `assetSlotBindings` to reserved/managed params in both edge and frontend controls-manifest validators.
4. **Reject** loose generated-component media params: `imageAssetKeys`, `videoAssetKeys`, `images`, and `videos` in schema/defaults/code for the code-path endpoint.
5. **Add** slot validation on the edge: duplicate slot ids, invalid ids, invalid media type, invalid cardinality, unknown binding slot, unknown asset key, wrong media type, missing required slots, and min/max violations.

## Phase 3: Frontend State, Save, Load, and Runtime Metadata

### Step 7: Carry asset slots through generated-component state (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/sequenceGenerationService.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/state/sequenceCreatorStore.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `RunSequenceComponentGenerationResult` and `GeneratedComponent` with normalized `assetSlots`.
2. **Keep** edited bindings in `generatedComponent.defaultsJson.assetSlotBindings` as the single source of truth for preview, smoke render, save, Insert, and Replace.
3. **Clear** stale generated component slot state when JSON-path drafts replace code-path state.

### Step 8: Save and reload slot metadata with resources (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSequenceResources.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Include** `assetSlots` in the `SequenceComponentMetadata` saved by `persistGeneratedComponent`.
2. **When loading or previewing** a library resource, populate `generatedComponent.assetSlots` from resource metadata.
3. **When reusing** a library component for Insert/Replace, synthesize the draft from current `generatedComponent.defaultsJson`, not raw resource defaults, so current slot picker assignments are applied.
4. **Do not** add MP4 preview cache fields, jobs, thumbnails, or resource metadata.

### Step 9: Make dynamic runtime materialization slot-aware (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/SequenceComponentRegistryContext.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** dynamic sequence registry entries to include `assetSlots` from each `SequenceComponentResource`.
2. **Plumb** dynamic entries or a `clipType -> assetSlots` lookup into materialization call sites that render DB-stored sequence components.
3. **Update** `materializeSequenceParams` / `materializeSequenceConfig` to accept optional slot metadata lookup while preserving current trusted descriptor-driven materialization for built-ins.
4. **Materialize** `params.assetSlotBindings` to `params.assetSlots` only when slot metadata is available for the clip type.
5. **Validate** slot id/cardinality/media type during materialization with registry media-type inference; warn and omit invalid URL injections rather than mutating persisted params.
6. **Remove** generic generated-component convention materialization from `imageAssetKeys` / `videoAssetKeys` to `images` / `videos`.

## Phase 4: Preview, Smoke Render, and Slot Picker UI

### Step 10: Update code-path preview to use slot bindings (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/CodePathPreview.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Accept** `assetSlots` and current `assetSlotBindings` in preview props.
2. **Resolve** bindings against `allowedAssets` using normalized media types and inject `params.assetSlots`.
3. **Stop** injecting `imageAssetKeys`, `assetKeys`, `assetUrls`, `images`, or `videos` fallbacks for generated components.
4. **Set** `clip.asset` from the first resolved slot URL only as compatibility data; generated component media rendering should use `params.assetSlots`.

### Step 11: Make smoke render slot-aware (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/headlessRender.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `smokeRenderSequenceComponent` input to accept pre-materialized params or `assetSlots` plus allowed asset registry.
2. **Before** calling the smoke gate in `persistGeneratedComponent`, materialize current `defaultsJson.assetSlotBindings` into `params.assetSlots` using current `allowedAssets` / `allowedRegistry`.
3. **Keep** the smoke gate strict: required slots must be valid before save-on-insert or save-on-replace proceeds.
4. **Add** tests proving a component that reads `params.assetSlots.hero[0]` can pass smoke render when the slot has a valid binding.

### Step 12: Build and wire slot pickers (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/AssetSlotPicker.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Render** one picker section per slot for generated and library components.
2. **Filter** candidates by slot media type using `AllowedSequenceAsset.mediaType`.
3. **Support** single-select when `maxItems === 1` and multi-select otherwise; reject duplicates by default.
4. **Show** inline required/min/max/wrong-media validation messages.
5. **Write** changes to `generatedComponent.defaultsJson.assetSlotBindings`.
6. **Disable** Insert/Replace for generated/library components when slot validation fails.
7. **Make** library Preview, generated Preview, Insert, and Replace all read current slot assignments.

### Step 13: Keep trusted JSON sequence editing stable (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/SequenceParamEditor.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Leave** trusted built-in `asset-list` params such as `image-jump.imageAssetKeys` and `resource-card.previewAssetKeys` working.
2. **Do not** migrate built-ins into `ASSET_SLOTS` in this pass.

## Phase 5: Tests and Verification

### Step 14: Add edge tests (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/sequence-component-validation.test.ts`, related template/extraction tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Assert** valid `ASSET_SLOTS` plus `DEFAULTS.assetSlotBindings` passes.
2. **Assert** code can read `params.assetSlots.hero` without declaring `assetSlots` in SCHEMA/DEFAULTS.
3. **Assert** controls validators allow `params.assetSlots` / `params.assetSlotBindings` outside `CONTROLS`.
4. **Assert** missing `ASSET_SLOTS`, duplicate slot ids, wrong media type, unknown keys, unknown slots, and cardinality failures are rejected.
5. **Assert** loose generated-component `imageAssetKeys` / `videoAssetKeys` / `images` / `videos` are rejected.

### Step 15: Add frontend tests (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.test.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/ControlsManifestLayout.test.tsx` or new picker tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Test** slot bindings materialize to URL arrays without mutating persisted params.
2. **Test** dynamic DB sequence materialization receives saved resource slot metadata and validates cardinality/media type.
3. **Test** generated and library slot picker changes update preview props and are used by Insert/Replace.
4. **Test** save-time smoke render receives materialized slot URLs.
5. **Test** trusted built-in sequence asset-list materialization remains covered.

### Step 16: Run focused validation, then broader checks
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** focused frontend tests first:
   ```bash
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx
   ```
2. **Run** Deno edge tests:
   ```bash
   deno test supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence-component/
   ```
3. **Run** build/type validation:
   ```bash
   npm run build
   ```
4. **Run** lint checks after behavior is green:
   ```bash
   npm run lint
   npm run lint:edge
   ```

## Execution Order
1. Land shared slot helpers and metadata types first.
2. Update edge allowed-asset normalization, prompt contract, parser, generated-code validator, and both controls-manifest validators before expecting model output to pass.
3. Wire frontend response/store/resource persistence after the edge shape is stable.
4. Plumb saved `assetSlots` metadata into dynamic runtime materialization before enforcing runtime slot validation.
5. Update preview and smoke render before enabling Insert/Replace gates.
6. Add slot picker UI last, using the helper validation already covered by tests.

## Validation Order
1. Start with helper and validator tests because they are cheapest and catch contract mistakes early.
2. Run materialization tests once dynamic slot metadata reaches the materializer.
3. Run Sequence Creator panel tests for generated preview, library preview, smoke render, Insert, and Replace.
4. Finish with edge Deno tests, `npm run build`, and lint checks.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 claims `updatepinnedgroupwithchildren` is 'currently imported by useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:357'. verified at useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:71-130 that this function is actually **defined locally** in useswitchtofinalvideo.ts, not imported from pinned-group-projection.ts. the real imports from pinned-group-projection at :5-8 are `buildgroupfromchildren` and `replacegroupchildren`, both of which are already in step 5's delete list. the plan's characterization is factually wrong (delete list shouldn't include updatepinnedgroupwithchildren as a pinned-group-projection export), but the substantive intent still works: once `buildgroupfromchildren`/`replacegroupchildren` are deleted, the local helper won't compile and step 11.2's 'inline soft-tag construction' rewrite removes it. minor wording error, not a blocking issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-132",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11.2 specifies the reverse switch is 'single commit, no `start`/`children`/`offset` reads anywhere' but doesn't state the mutation type explicitly. since the step describes metadeletes + cliporderoverride + pinnedshotgroupsoverride in a single commit, the mutation must be `type: 'rows'` (the only variant carrying all three). self-consistent with step 11.1's restriction on `type: 'pinnedshotgroups'`, but making the mutation type explicit would reduce executor ambiguity.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "roll-back-the-first-class-20260408-1337"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-135",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 3 against the actual `useselectedmediaclips()` inputs in [useselectedmediaclips.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). that hook currently reads only `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig` from editor context, so the plan's literal instruction to join `resolvedconfig.pinnedshotgroups` with `useshots()` data will not compile unless it first switches to `data?.config.pinnedshotgroups` or another real source of pinned groups.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-149",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual timeline selection ownership across `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelineselection.ts`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usemultiselect.ts`. `usetimelinecommit` owns only `selectedclipid` and `selectedtrackid`, while `usemultiselect` still owns `selectedclipids`, `primaryclipid`, and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan's step 3 is technically incomplete: following it literally would leave timeline selection split between the new store and old hook-local state instead of collapsing it into one authoritative store.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-150",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current imperative selection adapters in `useclipdrag.ts`, `usetimelinepersistence.ts`, `usepollsync.ts`, and `videoeditorprovider.tsx`. the mutable refs (`selectedclipidsref`, `additiveselectionref`, `selectedclipidref`, `selectedtrackidref`) are runtime adapters for drag/save/poll callers rather than durable domain state, so the plan's wording about putting 'refs/helpers' into the `timeline` slice is a design error unless it explicitly keeps store state separate from ref adapters rebuilt around that state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

    {
      "id": "DEBT-166",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` and `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/tracklistrenderer.tsx`. `timelinecanvas` still computes shared `rowresizepreview`, `selectedtrackid`, and other drag-sensitive props centrally and passes them through every `sortablerow`, so moving context reads to narrow zustand selectors alone will not meet the gate that non-dragged rows stay at 0 rerenders during drag unless the plan also changes this row-prop fan-out.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",
